In this module and hook "scrub" means UNSET (`\_GIT\_ENV\_VARS` popped at tools/tests/test\_pre\_push\_hook.py:64-70; `env -u ...` at .githooks/pre-push:119-128), and unsetting those two variables is precisely what makes git fall back to `~/.gitconfig`. The isolating form is `GIT\_CONFIG\_GLOBAL=/dev/null GIT\_CONFIG\_SYSTEM=/dev/null`, which `\_git()` (test\_pre\_push\_hook.py:73-74) already uses and which round one named; without it a script that reads effective scope passes on this machine and fails on any clone whose `~/.gitconfig` sets `core.sshCommand`, which is the environment-dependence round one blocked on; AC1/AC2 - having settled on LOCAL scope, the script will write a local `core.sshCommand` into a clone whose developer set a deliberate GLOBAL one, and local wins, so that setting is shadowed; the criteria do not require the message to say so (non-blocking, but the branch was chosen deliberately and the pusher should be told); carried from round one and accepted rather than acted on - AGENTS.md stays in `Affects` with no criterion covering the push paragraph (non-blocking); round-one findings ruled CLOSED and re-checked - AC3's mutant now edits `.githooks/pre-push` (the stderr cost line at :109, which the `\_Clone` fixture does drive as a subprocess and which is reached before the gate runs), AC3's text records that the fixture never runs `enable-hooks.sh` (confirmed: `\_Clone` copies the hook, `tools/gate\_timing.py` and a stub `gate.py`), AC1 is split so AC2 carries the unconditional-overwrite mutant that its own test would die on, both criteria now read `git config --local`, all three criteria are false at HEAD (no `ServerAlive`, `sshCommand` or keepalive text in tools/enable-hooks.sh, .githooks/pre-push, AGENTS.md or the test module), and `KeepaliveTests` collides with no existing class; BLOCKING: AC1 and AC2 - replace "scrubbed of `GIT\_CONFIG\_GLOBAL` and `GIT\_CONFIG\_SYSTEM`" with the mechanism that achieves the stated purpose, `GIT\_CONFIG\_GLOBAL=/dev/null` and `GIT\_CONFIG\_SYSTEM=/dev/null`; as written the criterion mandates the environment in which the developer's own `~/.gitconfig` DOES answer for the clone |

SUPERSEDED unit=BG0629 row-date=2026-08-27 row-verdict=REJECT row-reviewer=qa; subagent; plan-review r1 row-author=engineering; session authorised-by=operator (Darren Benson), asked and answered 2026-08-27 boundary=delivery of BG0629, RUN-01M11MEP reason=Both plan-review rejections of BG0629 were ANSWERED - the round-three review re-read every finding and ruled all five CLOSED, then APPROVED the plan. The rows cannot be retired by the shipped mechanism for the reason this unit exists: a later APPROVE must carry the rejection's brief fingerprint, and repairing what the reviewer rejected necessarily changes it. The repair record cannot discharge them either, because cmd\_repair resolves closures against the standing verdict alone while repair\_state counts across every unanswered rejection - which is this unit's own AC7. So the row that stands records a state that is no longer true, and no correct action can say so. Retired on the operator's explicit authority so the unit that repairs the wall can pass it. Disclosed at close. recorded=2026-08-27
